SmoothieBox Chapter 18 field-contact drawing; RotarySMP owner thread page 44, post #259199 (JMC drives in analog mode)

CONDITIONAL JASD v1.3 X/Z position command · SmoothieBox → AM26C31 → 5 V CN1 differential inputsSmoothieBox · X independent DRVX command fields · DRVX.5 · ENABLE · unused here, NO CONNECTIONNo conductor drawn. MANUFACTURER CONDITIONAL REFERENCE; not a verified fitted Schaublin drive, motor, cabinet or harness. Owner post #259199 says the JMC drives run in analog mode and considers temporary STEP/DIR position-mode tuning. Match both physical drive nameplates, revision and CN1 schedule to this recovered JASD v1.3 mirror before selecting this alternative. The owner-linked 1806080392.pdf is now unavailable; this mirror is not proven byte-identical. AM26C31 is a proposed four-channel 16-pin line-driver on an external adapter; its one common 0 V must be confirmed compatible with all four SmoothieBox DRVX/DRVZ GND fields. No 24 V pulse terminals, high-speed terminals or CN1 digital GND are joined to these differential pairs. Both enable pins select active outputs while 5 V is present; proposed 10 kΩ input pull-downs prevent floating CMOS inputs but do not constitute a safety stop, and rail ramp/boot/reset/disconnect pulse behavior requires bench proof with the motors inhibited. TI specifies 4.5–5.5 V supply, VIH≥2 V, VIL≤0.8 V, recommended ±20 mA output and a nearby 0.1 µF bypass. Manufacturer CN1 opto input with two 120 Ω resistors is shown in printed p.38; output current, polarity, edge timing, 500 kHz maximum, cable, EMC and receiver fit require measurement; no claim that the shown driver is automatically electrically qualified. P01-01=0, P03-00=0, P03-01=1, P03-02=0 are manufacturer position/STEP-DIR/low-speed selections, not fitted settings. DRVX/DRVZ ENABLE fields remain unconnected here; Servo ON is a distinct 24 V DI circuit. CN1 contact numbers below are manufacturer table positions only; no fabricated shell face/cavity geometry.

SmoothieBox Chapter 18 field-contact drawing; Owner Schaublin 125-CNC thread page 44, post #259199; JMC JASD v1.3 manufacturer-authored manual mirror; TI AM26C31 SLLS103P datasheet; Real SmoothieBox exterior source-contact contract

CONDITIONAL X JASD v1.3 power, matched motor and factory encoder boundary · three-phase manufacturer exampleX SAME CONDITIONAL JASD v1.3 servo drive · manufacturer named power terminals · B1 · regeneration terminal, OPEN pending exact modelNo conductor drawn. MANUFACTURER CONDITIONAL 220 V THREE-PHASE EXAMPLE FOR X, not an as-built supply, model, motor or cabinet diagram. The JASD v1.3 printed p.38 position example shows R/S/T main input downstream of a contactor, separate L1/L2 control supply, U/V/W motor power, drive/motor PE and CN2 absolute-encoder cable. Printed p.28 says main circuit R/S/T may have single/three-phase variants, R/T for 0.4 kW and below, L1/L2 single-phase 220 V and protective earth screw; the exact fitted drive nameplate decides whether this three-phase example is applicable. This reference includes an upstream protected three-phase main feed only as a qualified functional boundary; MCCB, filter, contactor ratings and location require cabinet survey/design, not values invented here. Keep the actual matched motor leads and encoder harness intact until both drive and motor identity, cable ratings and pinout are traced. The single OPEN CN2 record documents absent conductor-level pin schedule; it does not instruct disconnecting an existing cable. B1/B2/B3 resistor and brake handling remain unassigned until exact power size and fitted resistor are known. No control 0 V or PE is tied to motor phases, encoder return or 24 V Servo ON return.

SmoothieBox Chapter 18 field-contact drawing; JMC JASD v1.3 manufacturer-authored manual mirror

CONDITIONAL X/Z JASD v1.3 Servo ON · complete ULN2003AN / AQY212GS isolated 24 V process interfaceTI ULN2003AN · one 16-pin dual-channel PhotoMOS LED sink · pin 9 · clamp common NC for noninductive LED loadNo conductor drawn. MANUFACTURER CONDITIONAL PROCESS-ENABLE EXAMPLE, not a fitted Servo ON or safety circuit. The exact fitted drive must match the JASD v1.3 manual and each axis must read back P06-01=1 (DI1 Servo ON) with P06-00=0 (active low / optocoupler-on) before the contact path is selected. External qualified +24 V feeds each CN1.11 COM+; each CN1.9 DI1− returns through its own normally-open AQY212GS SOP4 contact to external 24 V 0 V. A single ULN2003AN channels 1 and 2 sinks two current-limited AQY LEDs from the independently qualified adapter +5 V rail. 390 Ω LED series resistor and 10 kΩ DRV ENABLE input pull-down per axis are proposed design values, not fitted parts; measure LED current over rail and temperature tolerance (Panasonic recommended 5–10 mA), ULN saturation, AQY contact off leakage/on resistance against actual JASD DI1 response, and SmoothieBox DRVX/DRVZ ENABLE logic voltage/current/polarity/boot/reset/disconnect before connection. ULN unused inputs 3–7 are defined low at logic ground; unused collector outputs 10–14 and clamp COM9 remain NC for LED loads. The separate AQY contact side is not bonded in this proposal to controller/ULN 0 V, pulse negatives, analog ground or PE; cabinet bonds must be traced because device isolation alone does not prove system isolation. This process Servo ON command does not replace independent E-stop, STO, brake, contactor or overtravel safety. CN1.17 +24 V auxiliary, CN1.45/.47/.49 COM returns, 24 V pulse alternatives and analog input are unused in this conditional process circuit; source inventory lists all 50 CN1 terminals without a fabricated connector face.

SmoothieBox Chapter 18 field-contact drawing; JMC JASD v1.3 manufacturer-authored manual mirror; TI ULN2003A Rev T datasheet; Panasonic AQY212GS SOP4 catalog pp.81–82; SmoothieBox real exterior source-contact contract

MANUFACTURER CONDITIONAL PROCESS-ENABLE EXAMPLE, not a fitted Servo ON or safety circuit. The exact fitted drive must match the JASD v1.3 manual and each axis must read back P06-01=1 (DI1 Servo ON) with P06-00=0 (active low / optocoupler-on) before the contact path is selected. External qualified +24 V feeds each CN1.11 COM+; each CN1.9 DI1− returns through its own normally-open AQY212GS SOP4 contact to external 24 V 0 V. A single ULN2003AN channels 1 and 2 sinks two current-limited AQY LEDs from the independently qualified adapter +5 V rail. 390 Ω LED series resistor and 10 kΩ DRV ENABLE input pull-down per axis are proposed design values, not fitted parts; measure LED current over rail and temperature tolerance (Panasonic recommended 5–10 mA), ULN saturation, AQY contact off leakage/on resistance against actual JASD DI1 response, and SmoothieBox DRVX/DRVZ ENABLE logic voltage/current/polarity/boot/reset/disconnect before connection. ULN unused inputs 3–7 are defined low at logic ground; unused collector outputs 10–14 and clamp COM9 remain NC for LED loads. The separate AQY contact side is not bonded in this proposal to controller/ULN 0 V, pulse negatives, analog ground or PE; cabinet bonds must be traced because device isolation alone does not prove system isolation. CONDITIONAL Lenze8214 isolated spindle speed · native PWM → low-pass → isolated0–10VPhoenix MINI MCR-2-UI-UI 2902037 · conditional 0–5 V → isolated 0–10 V · 7 · PWR+ duplicate · NO EXTERNAL CONNECTIONNo conductor drawn. Conditional design only: identify fitted drive and isolator revisions, verify Lenze0–10V jumper, measured PWM amplitude/frequency and startup defaults, low-pass component tolerances/loading/settling, configured voltage output and full-scale calibration. Manufacturer isolation rating must suit actual common-mode/cabinet; no reference bond across barrier or to Lenze39. Qualify open/short/power-loss behavior with drive inhibited before use. Native0–5V PWM uses carrier spindle connector, not a raw3.3V pin. 10kΩ/4.7µF is a proposed first-order passive filter; actualPWM≥20kHz and input>1Mohm are conditions, not fitted measurements. Factory isolator all-off config is CURRENT output0–20mA, not voltage: explicitly select/read back0–5V input and0–10V voltageoutput. Nominal24V power3/4 remains separate from signalinput1/2 and output5/6;7/8 are internally duplicatepower, not signal returns. Lenze7 GND1 must not be merged with39 GND2. This speed circuit does not include RUN/DIR or replace machine safety. Remaining Lenze terminals belong to separate digital/power inventories. Independent static review finds nominal47ms time constant, about216ms to1percent and ideal2.66mV peak-to-peak output ripple at20kHz/half duty. Loading permits approximately9.90V full-scale before module errors, so measure and calibrate rather than promise10V. An open resistor/reference can float the filter; an open capacitor removes filtering; charged capacitor can command speed on restart. Keep drive enable independently inhibited until valid zero command and stop behavior are established. This circuit is not fail-safe.

CONDITIONAL Lenze 8214 digital RUN/DIR · SmoothieBox → ULN2003AN → two Phoenix 24 V gold-signal relaysTI ULN2003AN · complete 16-pin two-channel relay-coil sink · pin 9 · clamp COM NC; Phoenix modules include freewheel diodesNo conductor drawn. OWNER-REPORTED DRIVE FAMILY / CONDITIONAL MANUFACTURER INTERFACE, not verified fitted nameplate or cabinet continuity. Owner post #338649 names Lenze 8214, terminal 28 enable, E4 direction, 39 digital reference and reports original Mesa connections. Manufacturer 8200 SHB 0199 indexed text (printed pp.4-38/4-40/4-41) gives terminal 28 and E4 HIGH 12–30 V, LOW 0–3 V; 39 is GND2, distinct from analog GND1 terminal 7. A separate nominal 24 V field supply (within the 12–30 V high range) feeds Phoenix PLC-RSC-24DC/21AU 2966265 intact-gold contacts 11→14; each Lenze input has a proposed 10 kΩ pull-down to digital 39 so an open NO contact has a defined LOW and a closed contact has ~2.4 mA resistor current at 24 V before input current, within the manufacturer's 1 mA minimum at 24 V / 50 mA maximum / 36 V DC maximum only if actual measured contact current and voltage meet the intact-gold ratings. The two Phoenix coils are separate host-side nominal 24 V loads at A1/A2, driven by ULN2003AN collectors 16/15; integrated freewheel/polarity diodes are source-confirmed, so observe A1-positive/A2-sink and do not add an invented external diode. Their 18.5–33.6 V 20°C coil operating range and typical 9 mA are source values, not host supply proof. Native SmoothieBox SSR1.1/.2 RUN and PWM.2 TTL/PWM.3 DIRECTION assignments are candidates: their actual output level/current, firmware mapping, polarity, turn-on/off and boot/reset/disconnect behavior must be measured before use. Two proposed 10 kΩ logic input pull-downs and ULN inputs 3–7 tied low leave unused collectors 10–14 and COM9 NC. Relay terminal 12 NC is unused. Phoenix's safe-isolation rating is a component source claim, not qualification of this fitted cabinet common-mode, installation or safety function. No host logic 0 V, coil 24 V return, field digital 39, analog 7, cabinet PE or Lenze internal terminal 20 is joined by label. E4 is factory CW LOW / CCW HIGH in the manual but programmable; verify fitted configuration and actual spindle rotation with independent safe stop. Process RUN/DIR relays do not replace hardwired E-stop, brake, contactor or protection. Manufacturer PDF bytes were not locally recovered (direct HTTP403); cited terminal claims are from indexed text, so final fitment and manual readback remain mandatory. Field 10 kΩ resistors are proposed at ≥0.25 W, with measured thermal margin; 30 V across 10 kΩ dissipates 0.09 W before tolerance. Do not change direction while RUN is asserted: command zero, verify standstill using the fitted drive procedure, inhibit RUN, set direction, and enable only after the qualified relay settling interval. Independent direction relay supply loss or disconnect may select CW while RUN remains asserted. Qualification must establish fault detection and drive inhibit before direction-control faults or changes, including relay bounce, settling and restart interlock. This proposed topology alone does not enforce that interlock. Verify coil pickup after ULN saturation and suppression transients over rated temperature/supply bounds; validate minimum contact switching load at the actual field voltage.

Process RUN/DIR relays do not replace hardwired E-stop, brake, contactor or protection. Field 10 kΩ resistors are proposed at ≥0.25 W, with measured thermal margin; 30 V across 10 kΩ dissipates 0.09 W before tolerance. Do not change direction while RUN is asserted: command zero, verify standstill using the fitted drive procedure, inhibit RUN, set direction, and enable only after the qualified relay settling interval. Independent direction relay supply loss or disconnect may select CW while RUN remains asserted. Qualification must establish fault detection and drive inhibit before direction-control faults or changes, including relay bounce, settling and restart interlock. This proposed topology alone does not enforce that interlock. Why this controller: Original machine uses separate motor power stages. Keep their motor-power/phase/encoder wiring; connect SmoothieBox exterior command contacts through the shown qualified interfaces. Chapter 18 is a proposed case harness, not an as-built rating certificate.

Earlier source compatibility diagram

Selected architecture: SmoothieBox is only a conditional command-controller proposal for the reported analog-mode JMC installation. Pulse-position compatibility remains OPEN; retain the supported LinuxCNC/Mesa architecture until exact drive identification and control-loop requirements permit conversion.

Original machine uses separate motor power stages. Keep their motor-power/phase/encoder wiring; connect SmoothieBox exterior command contacts through the shown qualified interfaces. Chapter 18 is a proposed case harness, not an as-built rating certificate. Selected architecture: SmoothieBox exterior case contacts with retained external power stages. The main diagram and connection-ID schedule define the current proposed routes. Dotted GUESS routes need the named commissioning checks; OPEN routes remain disconnected until the missing terminal, rating or interface is established. Previous Core alternatives remain only in the superseded historical architecture record.

Current installation sequence
  1. Match the actual machine and controller revisions, trace the unpowered harness and verify all signal/reference, power and independent safety boundaries before energising. Follow the current connection IDs rather than the superseded architecture prose.
  2. Retained owner-reported spindle circuit: Mesa 7i83 analogout0 → unknown VFD: SmoothieBox exterior contact assignment is a proposed Chapter 18 harness; verify the fitted case and logic level. Owner reports 7i83 analogout0 commands VFD speed. A later post identifies physical VFD Pin 28 as drive enable while discussing analog 0-10V speed command and stop behavior. Pin 28 is an owner-reported terminal label/function only; identify the VFD model and verify all actual connections. M3/M4 failure was reported even while panel buttons functioned.
  3. Owner-reported spindle encoder feedback; Smoothie compatibility blocked: SmoothieBox exterior contact assignment is a proposed Chapter 18 harness; verify the fitted case and logic level. Thread documents Mesa encoder speed feedback, not encoder model, A/B/Z electrical standard or connector schedule. No arbitrary direct encoder connection to SmoothieBox/Prime is proposed.
  4. JMC servo control compatibility decision (not a pin-level connection): SmoothieBox exterior contact assignment is a proposed Chapter 18 harness; verify the fitted case and logic level. Owner post #259199 explicitly says the JMC drives are running in analog mode. Either verify the exact installed drives can be configured for compatible pulse-position commands, or retain a supported closed-loop motion controller/drive architecture. No SmoothieBox-to-analog position loop is proposed.
  5. Pneumatic backgear: valve identity and stopped-spindle interlock required: SmoothieBox exterior contact assignment is a proposed Chapter 18 harness; verify the fitted case and logic level. Owner says gear changes only with spindle stopped, and no gear-position sensor is fitted/reported. Planned 7i84/brake outputs were not confirmed installed. Identify manifold station and coil; add independent spindle-stopped permissive before any actuation.
  6. Preserve existing physical spindle/safety controls until traced and verified: SmoothieBox exterior contact assignment is a proposed Chapter 18 harness; verify the fitted case and logic level. Thread reports damaged spare Mesa 7i84 and planned quick-stop/brake at cited time; current installation unknown. Do not label planned parts as installed. Keep safety and contactor logic independent of Smoothie. The named physical controls and safety functions are retained for tracing; no controller-to-contactor or forward-to-E-stop wire is proposed.
  7. Spindle speed and run · SmoothieBox interface proposal: Owner reports original 0–10V speed mode and VFD Pin28 enable. Identify fitted VFD model, analog range/return, enable circuit and safe stop behavior. Proposed PWM-to-0–10V conversion and isolated run contact are process control only; not a replacement for the retained spindle safety and closed-loop axis control.

X STEP logic command into AM26C31 X STEP channel
C07-001
GUESS
MAIN REFERENCE		SmoothieBox · X independent DRVX command fields · DRVX.1 · X STEPTI AM26C31 · one 16-pin four-channel differential adapter · all pins accounted · pin 1 · 1A · X STEP inputX STEP logic command into AM26C31 X STEP channel: SmoothieBox · X independent DRVX command fields · DRVX.1 · X STEP → TI AM26C31 · one 16-pin four-channel differential adapter · all pins accounted · pin 1 · 1A · X STEP input. This is a new conversion wire; the sources establish endpoint functions, while the connection and compatibility require the checks below.

Verify fitted SmoothieBox DRV logic VOH≥2 V, VOL≤0.8 V, source/sink capability, pulse width, polarity and disabled-motion startup before selecting JASD position mode.

  • SmoothieBox Chapter 18 field-contact drawing: Defines proposed exterior STEP/DIR/ENABLE, ground and accessory contact names. These field contacts are not Core header numbers. The drawing does not certify an assembled case or its output ratings.
  • Owner Schaublin 125-CNC thread page 44, post #259199: Actual JMC drives are reported in analog mode; owner discusses possible temporary STEP/DIR position-mode tuning, not fitted terminal identity.
  • JMC JASD v1.3 manufacturer-authored manual mirror: CN1 50-pin table, 5 V/24 V alternatives, printed pp.38–39 position wiring and 500 kHz limit, P01/P03 settings. Mirror not proven identical to owner-linked deleted file or fitted model.
  • TI AM26C31 SLLS103P datasheet: 16-pin four-channel differential driver top-view map, 4.5–5.5 V supply, 2 V VIH, 0.8 V VIL, ±20 mA recommended output, shared enables and 0.1 µF supply bypass.
  • Real SmoothieBox exterior source-contact contract: DRVX/DRVZ terminal names and source-art anchors. Does not qualify 5 V adapter supply, exact case output levels, current or installed harness.
  • SmoothieBox Chapter 18 DRV exterior contract; TI AM26C31 §5.3: Direct source reference is attached to this exact conductor; verify fitted applicability.

The earlier circuit details remain immediately below.

Earlier alternative retrofit architecture and circuit details

Owner retrofit guide · RotarySMP Schaublin 125-CNC owner retrofit

The owner reports JMC drives running in analog mode, with LinuxCNC/Mesa analog control and spindle encoder feedback. This is not a demonstrated drop-in Smoothie conversion. The main drawing separates the documented retained architecture from the missing drive-mode, VFD and feedback contracts. A pulse-position conversion requires exact drive identification, manufacturer confirmation and a new control design; otherwise retain the supported closed-loop controller.

Which Smoothie target fits this machine?
  • SmoothieBox: Enclosure/carrier has no terminal map for this evolving retrofit. Preserve the owner-reported Mesa/LINUXCNC source architecture until installed device schedules and requirements are known.
  • V2 Core: JMC AC servos are reported running in analog mode. A Core replacement is not established: first verify the exact drives can be configured for compatible pulse-position input. Otherwise retain a supported closed-loop controller; do not claim Core can close the analog position loop.
  • V2 Prime: Prime integrated coil outputs are not servo commands; this owner-reported JMC analog-servo/VFD arrangement is not a demonstrated Prime-compatible conversion.
Retain or add
  • No replacement part selected: identify exact JMC drive model/firmware and VFD model/manual before selecting signal interfaces.
  • Migration option A: if exact installed JMC drives support pulse-position mode, verify its terminals, differential/single-ended levels, timing, encoder/fault and control-loop behavior before considering Core.
  • Migration option B: if those drives cannot safely run from a compatible pulse-position controller, retain a supported closed-loop motion controller/servo architecture rather than assume a Smoothie analog position loop.
  • If spindle conversion is later considered, select a specific isolated analog-output plus enable/run interface from the exact VFD terminal schedule; preserve physical controls/E-stop.
Conversion and commissioning
  1. Record exact Mesa boards, JMC model labels/manual revisions, servo/encoder labels, VFD label/parameter setup, CPV manifold, brake, relay board, contactors and safety wiring.
  2. Use owner statement that the installed JMC drives are in analog mode as the control baseline. Inspect the current drive mode; do not generalize between JMC models.
  3. Check the exact JMC manual for supported analog and pulse-position modes. The forum links a JMC servo-drive PDF at post #259363, but without the installed model label it cannot be bound to this machine. Only proceed with Core if the exact drive can be deliberately reconfigured and the complete drive/controller loop is supported; otherwise retain a supported closed-loop controller.
  4. Resolve the reported M3/M4 behavior against current HAL/INI before claiming spindle control. Pin 28 is only a reported VFD enable terminal reference; verify model and function.
  5. Bench-check any VFD converter isolated from machine power; validate 0-10V range only if model manual proves it, common, run/enable logic, deceleration/brake and fail-safe state.
  6. Do not reconnect servos or VFD from a generic pin map. Confirm encoder feedback and independent safety before operation.
  7. Only change backgear with spindle stopped under independent permissive; verify engagement mechanically because owner reports no gear-position sensor.

Pin-by-pin schedule and commissioning notes
  • Owner thread reports Mesa 7i83 analogout0 used for VFD speed command; later owner report identifies VFD physical Pin 28 as drive enable during discussion of 0-10V speed command. Pin 28 is not a complete VFD schedule and exact model remains unknown.
  • Owner post #259199 (2022-12-11) explicitly reports “I am running JMC drives in analog mode.” It does not name the exact drive models or terminal pins. Post #259363 links a JMC servo-drive manual, but source applicability to the installed unidentified drives is unproven. Do not draw Core STEP/DIR to an analog-mode drive or invent a Core analog closed-loop path.
  • No physical terminal numbers sourced for JMC command/enable/alarm/encoder, VFD analog range/common/run/brake, spindle encoder A/B/Z/power, CPV coil, brake, contactors or E-stop. Mark OPEN until model-specific documentation and as-built tracing are available.
  • The forum states a spare 7i84 was damaged/not installed at a cited time and brake/quick-stop were planned; current state is unknown. Physical forward/reverse/stop reportedly worked when M3/M4 commands failed.

  1. Forum thread · Open cited source 1

Source update · drive identity and conditional JMC position-mode reference · 2026-10-02

This dated research describes the earlier diagram and preserves its historical state. The current primary reference shows 122 conditional connections ahead of the retained 32-connection compatibility main. Neither the research nor the current proposal certifies installed hardware, fitted wiring or unresolved machine interfaces.

Owner-reported spindle-drive details

In post #338649, the owner names a Lenze 8214 VFD and an EMB 9351-E brake module, and reports a 0–10 V jumper, terminal 28 enable, E4 direction, and terminal 39 ground. The post also describes terminals 9, 20, and 62 as unused and K11 NO/K12 COM as Mesa inputs. These are owner-reported labels and connections, not independently verified fitted-drive continuity; the exact manual polarity and ratings for these points remain unconfirmed. This newer identification qualifies the earlier diagram’s “VFD model unknown” statement as an earlier evidence boundary; it does not turn the diagram into an as-built wiring map.

Conditional JMC servo position-mode reference

Owner post #259199 says the JMC drives were running in analog mode and discusses considering STEP/DIR position mode for tuning. The post’s linked manual file in #259363 is unavailable at its former download address. The available source is a manufacturer-authored JASD v1.3 manual mirror; it is not proven identical to that linked file and does not identify the installed drive model.

  • The manual’s 5 V pulse/direction reference uses CN1.43 pulse+, CN1.41 pulse−, CN1.36 direction+, and CN1.37 direction−. Its separate 24 V reference uses CN1.39 pulse+ and CN1.35 direction+ with the corresponding negative inputs; these are distinct input modes, not interchangeable terminals. See the manual’s input diagrams (PDF page 40, printed page 39) and CN1 contact table (PDF page 22, printed page 21).
  • The manual lists P01-01=0, P03-00=0, P03-01=1, and P03-02=0 as reference position-mode values on PDF page 41. These are not asserted as settings for an unidentified fitted unit.

A converted position-mode circuit is being prepared only as a conditional proposal; it is not installed and is not shown in the current drawing. This source review does not establish an analog-DAC position-loop substitute or synchronized-threading capability. Identify the fitted drive and revision, match its manual, trace the harness, and qualify the command interface before any conversion.

Source update · Lenze 8214 analog and isolated control references · 2026-10-02

The owner identifies a Lenze 8214 in post #338649. The indexed text for the Lenze 8200 SHB 0199 manual provides conditional family-level references below; direct retrieval was blocked, so this note is based on indexed manual text, without a retained PDF hash or page-image inspection. Applicability remains conditional on the fitted unit matching the 8214.

  • The manual text identifies terminal 7 as GND1 (analog reference) and terminal 8 as the 0–10 V analog input, with jumper 1–2 specified in the text; verify the physical jumper before use. Terminal 39 is GND2, an external digital reference. The manual indicates GND1 and GND2 may be isolated within the unit, so do not assume they are interchangeable.
  • For terminals 28 and E4, the text gives HIGH as 12–30 V and LOW as 0–3 V. This supports use only with a separately qualified control source; it does not establish compatibility with a direct 3.3 V host signal. The manual distinguishes 821X terminal 20 supply as 15 V / 20 mA from the 820X family; the owner reports terminal 20 unused, and the 821X value must not be transferred to an 820X unit.
  • The manual describes basic isolation for 820X/821X control terminals and calls for a second insulating distance in connected components for contact protection. Direct host-ground or DAC transfer is therefore not justified by this reference; a qualified isolation interface is being designed, while the current drawing remains pending.
  • The manual text labels K11 as NC at its relay contact, while the owner post calls K11 NO. Trace the fitted relay model, function, and continuity; do not resolve this difference by assuming a fault or output lead.

These are conditional manufacturer-manual references and owner-reported labels, not a verified installed circuit or approved connection schedule.

Manufacturer reference · JASD v1.3 CN1 terminal inventory · 50 entries

Source-only CN1 table · 2026-10-02

This is a transcription of the manufacturer-authored JASD v1.3 manual mirror’s CN1 table (printed pages 20–21). It records source terminal numbers, labels, and descriptions only. The mirror is not byte-verified against the owner-linked original PDF, and it does not identify the fitted drive or establish a connector-face orientation, mating geometry, harness population, or cabinet use. “Not selected in reference as built unknown” is a research disposition; it does not mean the terminal is absent or disconnected on the machine. The proposed servo interface and drawing remain pending; this inventory makes no retrofit claim.

Lenze 8200 SHB 0199 indexed source inventory · 19 known control-terminal entries

Indexed manufacturer text · bounded terminal inventory · 2026-10-02

The owner identifies a Lenze 8214 in post #338649. This reference inventory records 19 control-terminal entries recovered from indexed text for the Lenze 8200 SHB 0199 family manual; it is not a full cabinet terminal census or connector-face map. Direct PDF retrieval returned HTTP 403. No PDF bytes, native hash, or page pixels were recovered, so this is indexed-text evidence only. The source covers multiple model families and does not prove which optional terminals are physically populated on this 8214.

Preserve the source distinctions: terminal 7 GND1 is the analog reference, terminal 39 GND2 the digital return, and terminal 20 is an 821X internal 15 V / 20 mA source reported unused. The K1 block conflicts: indexed table OCR gives K22/K24 while the printed strip overview labels K11/K12/K14. K11 also conflicts between manual table NC/TRIP and owner-reported NO. Do not normalize either difference or assign a relay/fault function without matching the fitted unit and tracing continuity. K2, thermal terminals, and the terminal 8 input mode remain model-dependent or require physical verification.

The proposed isolated speed and RUN/DIR branches are still conditional. The current candidate adds 114 proposed conductor records across the alternative circuits; full diagrams remain pending acceptance. None of these records establish installed wiring.
